⏺ I'll create a simple Go CLI program to validate the JSON file.                                                                       10:50 AM  claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929

⏺ Write(/Users/choonkeat/git/chinese-flashcards/cmd/validate/main.go)
  ⎿  Wrote 56 lines to /Users/choonkeat/git/chinese-flashcards/cmd/validate/main.go
     package main

     import (
       "encoding/json"
       "fmt"
       "os"
       "strings"
     )

     type Flashcard struct {
       Word             string `json:"word"`
       WordHanyupinyin  string `json:"word_hanyupinyin"`
       WordEnglish      string `json:"word_english"`
       Sentence         string `json:"sentence"`
       SentenceHanyu    string `json:"sentence_hanyupinyin"`
       SentenceEnglish  string `json:"sentence_english"`
     }

     func main() {
       if len(os.Args) < 2 {
         fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Usage: %s <json-file>\n", os.Args[0])
         os.Exit(1)
       }

       filename := os.Args[1]
       data, err := os.ReadFile(filename)
       if err != nil {
         fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Error reading file: %v\n", err)
         os.Exit(1)
       }

       var flashcards []Flashcard
       if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &flashcards); err != nil {
         fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Error parsing JSON: %v\n", err)
         os.Exit(1)
       }

       invalidCount := 0
       for i, card := range flashcards {
         if !strings.Contains(card.Sentence, card.Word) {
           invalidCount++
           fmt.Printf("Entry %d: word '%s' (%s) not found in sentence '%s'\n",
             i+1, card.Word, card.WordEnglish, card.Sentence)
         }
       }

       fmt.Printf("\n--- Summary ---\n")
       fmt.Printf("Total entries: %d\n", len(flashcards))
       fmt.Printf("Invalid entries: %d\n", invalidCount)
       fmt.Printf("Valid entries: %d\n", len(flashcards)-invalidCount)

       if invalidCount > 0 {
         os.Exit(1)
       }
     }

∴ Thinking…

  The user wants me to describe the JSON schema we've been using for the flashcards in TypeScript notation. Let me look at the structure we've been using.

  From the flashcard entries, the schema is:
  - word: string (Chinese character)
  - word_hanyupinyin: string (pinyin with tone marks)
  - word_english: string (English translation)
  - sentence: string (Chinese sentence using the word)
  - sentence_hanyupinyin: string (pinyin for the sentence)
  - sentence_english: string (English translation of sentence)

  The file is an array of these objects.

⏺ interface Flashcard {                                                                                                                10:55 AM  claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929
    word: string;              // Chinese character (e.g., "你")
    word_hanyupinyin: string;  // Pinyin with tone marks (e.g., "nǐ")
    word_english: string;      // English translation (e.g., "you")
    sentence: string;          // Chinese sentence using the word (e.g., "你好！")
    sentence_hanyupinyin: string; // Pinyin for sentence (e.g., "Nǐ hǎo!")
    sentence_english: string;  // English translation (e.g., "Hello!")
  }

  type FlashcardList = Flashcard[];

> Write a simple static claude/index.html 
1. User can choose a local json file to import into the web page
2. The json file must be of the following schema, otherwise show an error messge

  interface Flashcard {
    word: string;              // Chinese character (e.g., "你")
    word_hanyupinyin: string;  // Pinyin with tone marks (e.g., "nǐ")
    word_english: string;      // English translation (e.g., "you")
    sentence: string;          // Chinese sentence using the word (e.g., "你好！")
    sentence_hanyupinyin: string; // Pinyin for sentence (e.g., "Nǐ hǎo!")
    sentence_english: string;  // English translation (e.g., "Hello!")
  }

  type FlashcardList = Flashcard[];

    i.e. the json file is a big json array

3. The parsed file data is stored into localStorage. Below the <input type=file>, we show how many words are in localStorage and provide a little trash icon to clear it
4. Uploading another file will append to this data; duplicate word is discarded

Clicking “Play”, the webpage will pick the next scheduled word to play. If the user gets it wrong, the word gets scheduled to be retried soon. If the user gets it correct, the
 word gets scheduled to be retried later. The next word appears and the logic repeats. We want to implement the delays such that it is in line with how spaced repetition is 
done.

How does a user “play” a card? There are actually 2 ways to play a card, and we should record the tries/success/failure separately. 

For each flashcard, we must test whether the User can “read” and “write”. 

Testing “read”
1. It will display flashcard.word as title and flashcard.sentence as body.
2. There is a “I don’t know” button: when tapped, use browser text to speech to speak flashcard.sentence aloud 1 time at 0.7x speed, and 1 more time in 1x speed. Then reveal 
“Next card” button
3. Tapping “Next card” button will go to next card
4. It will wait for the User to pronounce the sentence — I think we can detect User pronouncing the sentence by using the browser’s speech to text keyboard (please suggest) 
and type into a text field (auto focus).
5. If the words did not match, reveal the flashcard.word_hanyupinyin below flashcard.word, flashcard.sentence_hanyupinyin below flashcard.sentence, clear the speech to text 
input and loop back to (4)
6. If the speech to text words matches flashcard.sentence, then it is correct and we show a confetti effect along with revealing the “Next card” button

Testing “write”
1. It will display flashcard.word_english as title and flashcard.sentence_english as body.
2. There is a “Show answer” button: when tapped, reveal flashcard.word + flashcard.word_hanyupinyin below flashcard.word_english and flashcard.sentence + 
flashcard.sentence_hanyupinyin below flashcard.sentence_english.
3. Then reveal “Correct” and “Wrong” button; tapping either will proceed to next card
4. Tapping “Correct” will show a confetti effect before proceeding to next card 

∴ Thinking…

  This is a fairly complex single-page application. Let me break down the requirements:

  1. File upload to import JSON flashcards
  2. Validate JSON schema
  3. Store in localStorage, show count, trash icon to clear
  4. Append on upload, dedupe by word
  5. Spaced repetition scheduling
  6. Two modes: "read" and "write" testing
  7. Read mode: display word/sentence, speech-to-text for user input, text-to-speech for hints
  8. Write mode: show English, user self-grades
  9. Confetti effects on success

  Let me create a comprehensive single HTML file with all the necessary JavaScript and CSS.

  For spaced repetition, I'll implement a simple SM-2 like algorithm with intervals that increase on success and reset on failure.

  For speech-to-text, I'll use the Web Speech API (SpeechRecognition).
  For text-to-speech, I'll use the Web Speech API (SpeechSynthesis).

  Let me structure the HTML file:
  - CSS styles
  - HTML structure
  - JavaScript logic

  Key data structures:
  - Flashcards stored in localStorage
  - Progress tracking per card (separate for read/write)
  - Scheduling based on spaced repetition intervals

  I'll need to track for each word:
  - read: { nextReview: timestamp, interval: number, successCount: number, failCount: number }
  - write: { nextReview: timestamp, interval: number, successCount: number, failCount: number }

  Spaced repetition intervals (in minutes for testing, could be days in production):
  - Initial: 1 min
  - After 1 success: 10 min
  - After 2 successes: 1 hour
  - After 3 successes: 1 day
  - After 4 successes: 3 days
  - After 5+ successes: 7 days
  - On failure: reset to 1 min

  Let me write this out.

       <script>
         // Storage keys
         const STORAGE_KEYS = {
           FLASHCARDS: 'flashcards',
           PROGRESS: 'progress'
         };

         // Spaced repetition intervals (in milliseconds)
         const INTERVALS = [
           1 * 60 * 1000,        // 1 minute
           10 * 60 * 1000,       // 10 minutes
           60 * 60 * 1000,       // 1 hour
           24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,  // 1 day
           3 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,  // 3 days
           7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,  // 7 days
           14 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000, // 14 days
           30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000  // 30 days
         ];

         // State
         let flashcards = [];
         let progress = {};
         let currentCard = null;
         let currentMode = 'read'; // 'read' or 'write'
         let recognition = null;

         // DOM elements
         const fileInput = document.getElementById('fileInput');
         const wordCountEl = document.getElementById('wordCount');
         const clearBtn = document.getElementById('clearBtn');
         const errorMsg = document.getElementById('errorMsg');
         const playBtn = document.getElementById('playBtn');
         const homeScreen = document.getElementById('homeScreen');
         const gameScreen = document.getElementById('gameScreen');
         const backBtn = document.getElementById('backBtn');
         const modeIndicator = document.getElementById('modeIndicator');
         const homeStats = document.getElementById('homeStats');
         const gameStats = document.getElementById('gameStats');

         // Read mode elements
         const readMode = document.getElementById('readMode');
         const readWord = document.getElementById('readWord');
         const readPinyin = document.getElementById('readPinyin');
         const readSentence = document.getElementById('readSentence');
         const readSentencePinyin = document.getElementById('readSentencePinyin');
         const readInstruction = document.getElementById('readInstruction');
         const micBtn = document.getElementById('micBtn');
         const speechInput = document.getElementById('speechInput');
         const dontKnowBtn = document.getElementById('dontKnowBtn');
         const readNextBtn = document.getElementById('readNextBtn');

         // Write mode elements
         const writeMode = document.getElementById('writeMode');
         const writeWordEnglish = document.getElementById('writeWordEnglish');
         const writeWordChinese = document.getElementById('writeWordChinese');
         const writeSentenceEnglish = document.getElementById('writeSentenceEnglish');
         const writeSentenceChinese = document.getElementById('writeSentenceChinese');
         const showAnswerBtn = document.getElementById('showAnswerBtn');
         const writeShowGroup = document.getElementById('writeShowGroup');
         const writeAnswerGroup = document.getElementById('writeAnswerGroup');
         const wrongBtn = document.getElementById('wrongBtn');
         const correctBtn = document.getElementById('correctBtn');

         // Initialize
         function init() {
           loadFromStorage();
           updateUI();
           setupEventListeners();
           setupSpeechRecognition();
         }

         function loadFromStorage() {
           const storedCards = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEYS.FLASHCARDS);
           const storedProgress = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEYS.PROGRESS);

           if (storedCards) {
             flashcards = JSON.parse(storedCards);
           }
           if (storedProgress) {
             progress = JSON.parse(storedProgress);
           }
         }

         function saveToStorage() {
           localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEYS.FLASHCARDS, JSON.stringify(flashcards));
           localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEYS.PROGRESS, JSON.stringify(progress));
         }

         function updateUI() {
           wordCountEl.textContent = `${flashcards.length} words`;
           playBtn.disabled = flashcards.length === 0;
           updateHomeStats();
         }

         function updateHomeStats() {
           const stats = calculateStats();
           homeStats.innerHTML = `
             <span>Read: ${stats.read.mastered}/${flashcards.length} mastered</span>
             <span>Write: ${stats.write.mastered}/${flashcards.length} mastered</span>
           `;
         }

         function calculateStats() {
           let readMastered = 0;
           let writeMastered = 0;

           flashcards.forEach(card => {
             const p = progress[card.word];
             if (p) {
               if (p.read && p.read.intervalIndex >= 4) readMastered++;
               if (p.write && p.write.intervalIndex >= 4) writeMastered++;
             }
           });

           return {
             read: { mastered: readMastered },
             write: { mastered: writeMastered }
           };
         }

         function setupEventListeners() {
           fileInput.addEventListener('change', handleFileUpload);
           clearBtn.addEventListener('click', handleClear);
           playBtn.addEventListener('click', startGame);
           backBtn.addEventListener('click', endGame);

           // Read mode
           micBtn.addEventListener('click', toggleSpeechRecognition);
           dontKnowBtn.addEventListener('click', handleDontKnow);
           readNextBtn.addEventListener('click', () => nextCard(false));

           // Write mode
           showAnswerBtn.addEventListener('click', handleShowAnswer);
           wrongBtn.addEventListener('click', () => handleWriteAnswer(false));
           correctBtn.addEventListener('click', () => handleWriteAnswer(true));
         }

         function validateFlashcard(card) {
           const requiredFields = ['word', 'word_hanyupinyin', 'word_english', 'sentence', 'sentence_hanyupinyin', 'sentence_english'];
           return requiredFields.every(field => typeof card[field] === 'string' && card[field].length > 0);
         }

         function handleFileUpload(e) {
           const file = e.target.files[0];
           if (!file) return;

           const reader = new FileReader();
           reader.onload = (event) => {
             try {
               const data = JSON.parse(event.target.result);

               if (!Array.isArray(data)) {
                 throw new Error('JSON must be an array of flashcards');
               }

               const invalidCards = data.filter((card, i) => !validateFlashcard(card));
               if (invalidCards.length > 0) {
                 throw new Error(`Invalid flashcard schema. Each card must have: word, word_hanyupinyin, word_english, sentence, sentence_hanyupinyin, sentence_english`);
               }

               // Merge with existing, dedupe by word
               const existingWords = new Set(flashcards.map(c => c.word));
               const newCards = data.filter(card => !existingWords.has(card.word));
               flashcards = [...flashcards, ...newCards];

               saveToStorage();
               updateUI();

               errorMsg.style.display = 'none';
               if (newCards.length < data.length) {
                 showError(`Added ${newCards.length} new words (${data.length - newCards.length} duplicates skipped)`, false);
               }
             } catch (err) {
               showError(err.message);
             }
           };
           reader.readAsText(file);
           fileInput.value = '';
         }

         function showError(msg, isError = true) {
           errorMsg.textContent = msg;
           errorMsg.style.display = 'block';
           errorMsg.style.background = isError ? '#ffebee' : '#e8f5e9';
           errorMsg.style.color = isError ? '#d32f2f' : '#2e7d32';
         }

         function handleClear() {
           if (confirm('Clear all flashcards and progress?')) {
             flashcards = [];
             progress = {};
             localStorage.removeItem(STORAGE_KEYS.FLASHCARDS);
             localStorage.removeItem(STORAGE_KEYS.PROGRESS);
             updateUI();
           }
         }

         function getNextScheduledCard() {
           const now = Date.now();
           let candidates = [];

           flashcards.forEach(card => {
             const p = progress[card.word] || { read: null, write: null };

             // Check read
             const readNext = p.read?.nextReview || 0;
             if (readNext <= now) {
               candidates.push({ card, mode: 'read', priority: readNext });
             }

             // Check write
             const writeNext = p.write?.nextReview || 0;
             if (writeNext <= now) {
               candidates.push({ card, mode: 'write', priority: writeNext });
             }
           });

           if (candidates.length === 0) {
             // All cards reviewed, find soonest
             let soonest = null;
             flashcards.forEach(card => {
               const p = progress[card.word] || { read: null, write: null };
               const readNext = p.read?.nextReview || Infinity;
               const writeNext = p.write?.nextReview || Infinity;
               const next = Math.min(readNext, writeNext);
               if (!soonest || next < soonest.priority) {
                 soonest = { card, mode: readNext <= writeNext ? 'read' : 'write', priority: next };
               }
             });
             return soonest;
           }

           // Sort by priority (oldest first) and pick randomly from top candidates
           candidates.sort((a, b) => a.priority - b.priority);
           const topPriority = candidates[0].priority;
           const topCandidates = candidates.filter(c => c.priority === topPriority);
           return topCandidates[Math.floor(Math.random() * topCandidates.length)];
         }

         function startGame() {
           homeScreen.classList.add('hidden');
           gameScreen.classList.remove('hidden');
           nextCard(true);
         }

         function endGame() {
           gameScreen.classList.add('hidden');
           homeScreen.classList.remove('hidden');
           stopSpeechRecognition();
           updateUI();
         }

         function nextCard(isFirst = false) {
           const next = getNextScheduledCard();
           if (!next) {
             alert('No cards to review!');
             endGame();
             return;
           }

           currentCard = next.card;
           currentMode = next.mode;

           resetCardUI();

           if (currentMode === 'read') {
             showReadMode();
           } else {
             showWriteMode();
           }

           updateGameStats();
         }

         function resetCardUI() {
           // Read mode reset
           readPinyin.classList.add('hidden');
           readSentencePinyin.classList.add('hidden');
           readNextBtn.classList.add('hidden');
           dontKnowBtn.classList.remove('hidden');
           speechInput.value = '';
           speechInput.classList.remove('listening');
           micBtn.classList.remove('listening');
           readInstruction.textContent = 'Pronounce the sentence above';

           // Write mode reset
           writeWordChinese.classList.add('hidden');
           writeSentenceChinese.classList.add('hidden');
           writeShowGroup.classList.remove('hidden');
           writeAnswerGroup.classList.add('hidden');
         }

         function showReadMode() {
           modeIndicator.textContent = 'READ';
           modeIndicator.className = 'mode-indicator read';
           readMode.style.display = 'block';
           writeMode.style.display = 'none';

           readWord.textContent = currentCard.word;
           readPinyin.textContent = currentCard.word_hanyupinyin;
           readSentence.textContent = currentCard.sentence;
           readSentencePinyin.textContent = currentCard.sentence_hanyupinyin;
         }

         function showWriteMode() {
           modeIndicator.textContent = 'WRITE';
           modeIndicator.className = 'mode-indicator write';
           readMode.style.display = 'none';
           writeMode.style.display = 'block';

           writeWordEnglish.textContent = currentCard.word_english;
           writeWordChinese.textContent = `${currentCard.word} (${currentCard.word_hanyupinyin})`;
           writeSentenceEnglish.textContent = currentCard.sentence_english;
           writeSentenceChinese.textContent = `${currentCard.sentence} (${currentCard.sentence_hanyupinyin})`;
         }

         function updateGameStats() {
           const p = progress[currentCard.word] || { read: {}, write: {} };
           const modeProgress = p[currentMode] || {};
           gameStats.innerHTML = `
             <span>Success: ${modeProgress.successCount || 0}</span>
             <span>Fail: ${modeProgress.failCount || 0}</span>
             <span>Level: ${(modeProgress.intervalIndex || 0) + 1}/8</span>
           `;
         }

         // Speech Recognition
         function setupSpeechRecognition() {
           if ('webkitSpeechRecognition' in window) {
             recognition = new webkitSpeechRecognition();
             recognition.continuous = true;
             recognition.interimResults = true;
             recognition.lang = 'zh-CN';

             recognition.onresult = (event) => {
               let transcript = '';
               for (let i = event.resultIndex; i < event.results.length; i++) {
                 transcript += event.results[i][0].transcript;
               }
               speechInput.value = transcript;
               checkSpeechMatch(transcript);
             };

             recognition.onerror = (event) => {
               console.error('Speech recognition error:', event.error);
               stopSpeechRecognition();
             };

             recognition.onend = () => {
               if (micBtn.classList.contains('listening')) {
                 // Restart if still supposed to be listening
                 recognition.start();
               }
             };
           }
         }

         function toggleSpeechRecognition() {
           if (!recognition) {
             alert('Speech recognition not supported in this browser. Please use Chrome.');
             return;
           }

           if (micBtn.classList.contains('listening')) {
             stopSpeechRecognition();
           } else {
             startSpeechRecognition();
           }
         }

         function startSpeechRecognition() {
           if (!recognition) return;
           try {
             recognition.start();
             micBtn.classList.add('listening');
             speechInput.classList.add('listening');
             readInstruction.textContent = 'Listening... Speak the sentence';
           } catch (e) {
             console.error('Failed to start recognition:', e);
           }
         }

         function stopSpeechRecognition() {
           if (!recognition) return;
           try {
             recognition.stop();
           } catch (e) {}
           micBtn.classList.remove('listening');
           speechInput.classList.remove('listening');
         }

         function normalizeText(text) {
           // Remove punctuation and whitespace for comparison
           return text.replace(/[\s\.,!?，。！？、]/g, '').toLowerCase();
         }

         function checkSpeechMatch(transcript) {
           const expected = normalizeText(currentCard.sentence);
           const actual = normalizeText(transcript);

           if (actual.includes(expected) || expected.includes(actual) && actual.length >= expected.length * 0.8) {
             // Match found!
             stopSpeechRecognition();
             handleReadSuccess();
           }
         }

         function handleReadSuccess() {
           recordResult(true);
           showConfetti();
           readInstruction.textContent = 'Correct!';
           readPinyin.classList.remove('hidden');
           readSentencePinyin.classList.remove('hidden');
           readNextBtn.classList.remove('hidden');
           dontKnowBtn.classList.add('hidden');
         }

         function handleDontKnow() {
           stopSpeechRecognition();
           recordResult(false);

           // Reveal pinyin
           readPinyin.classList.remove('hidden');
           readSentencePinyin.classList.remove('hidden');

           // Speak the sentence
           speakSentence(currentCard.sentence, 0.7, () => {
             speakSentence(currentCard.sentence, 1.0, () => {
               readNextBtn.classList.remove('hidden');
               dontKnowBtn.classList.add('hidden');
               readInstruction.textContent = 'Listen and repeat';
             });
           });
         }

         function speakSentence(text, rate, callback) {
           const utterance = new SpeechSynthesisUtterance(text);
           utterance.lang = 'zh-CN';
           utterance.rate = rate;
           utterance.onend = callback;
           speechSynthesis.speak(utterance);
         }

         function handleShowAnswer() {
           writeWordChinese.classList.remove('hidden');
           writeSentenceChinese.classList.remove('hidden');
           writeShowGroup.classList.add('hidden');
           writeAnswerGroup.classList.remove('hidden');
         }

         function handleWriteAnswer(isCorrect) {
           recordResult(isCorrect);
           if (isCorrect) {
             showConfetti();
             setTimeout(() => nextCard(), 1000);
           } else {
             nextCard();
           }
         }

         function recordResult(success) {
           if (!progress[currentCard.word]) {
             progress[currentCard.word] = { read: null, write: null };
           }

           let modeProgress = progress[currentCard.word][currentMode];
           if (!modeProgress) {
             modeProgress = {
               intervalIndex: 0,
               nextReview: 0,
               successCount: 0,
               failCount: 0
             };
           }

           if (success) {
             modeProgress.successCount++;
             modeProgress.intervalIndex = Math.min(modeProgress.intervalIndex + 1, INTERVALS.length - 1);
           } else {
             modeProgress.failCount++;
             modeProgress.intervalIndex = 0; // Reset to beginning
           }

           modeProgress.nextReview = Date.now() + INTERVALS[modeProgress.intervalIndex];
           progress[currentCard.word][currentMode] = modeProgress;

           saveToStorage();
           updateGameStats();
         }

         // Confetti
         function showConfetti() {
           const canvas = document.getElementById('confettiCanvas');
           const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
           canvas.width = window.innerWidth;
           canvas.height = window.innerHeight;

           const particles = [];
           const colors = ['#ff0000', '#00ff00', '#0000ff', '#ffff00', '#ff00ff', '#00ffff'];

           for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
             particles.push({
               x: canvas.width / 2,
               y: canvas.height / 2,
               vx: (Math.random() - 0.5) * 20,
               vy: (Math.random() - 0.5) * 20 - 10,
               color: colors[Math.floor(Math.random() * colors.length)],
               size: Math.random() * 8 + 4,
               life: 1
             });
           }

           function animate() {
             ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

             let alive = false;
             particles.forEach(p => {
               if (p.life <= 0) return;
               alive = true;

               p.x += p.vx;
               p.y += p.vy;
               p.vy += 0.5; // gravity
               p.life -= 0.02;

               ctx.globalAlpha = p.life;
               ctx.fillStyle = p.color;
               ctx.fillRect(p.x, p.y, p.size, p.size);
             });

             if (alive) {
               requestAnimationFrame(animate);
             } else {
               ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
             }
           }

           animate();
         }

         // Start the app
         init();
       </script>
     </body>
     </html>

     # Chinese Flashcards - Developer Guide

     ## Overview

     A static single-page web application for learning Chinese characters using spaced repetition. Built with vanilla HTML, CSS, and JavaScript - no build tools required.

     ## Project Structure

     ```
     claude/
     ├── index.html          # Main application (all HTML, CSS, JS in one file)
     ├── DEVELOPER.md        # This file
     └── SPACED_REPETITION.md # Spaced repetition algorithm documentation
     ```

     ## Running the Application

     Start a local HTTP server from the project root:

     ```bash
     cd /Users/choonkeat/git/chinese-flashcards
     python3 -m http.server 8765
     ```

     Then open: http://localhost:8765/claude/

     ## Testing with MCP Playwright

     The app can be tested using MCP Playwright tools. Example workflow:

     ```javascript
     // 1. Navigate to the app
     mcp__playwright__browser_navigate({ url: "http://localhost:8765/claude/" })

     // 2. Upload flashcard JSON file
     mcp__playwright__browser_click({ element: "Choose File button", ref: "e7" })
     mcp__playwright__browser_file_upload({
       paths: ["/Users/choonkeat/git/chinese-flashcards/2015characterlistprimarychinese.pdf.json"]
     })

     // 3. Click Play to start
     mcp__playwright__browser_click({ element: "Play button", ref: "e12" })

     // 4. Take snapshots to verify UI state
     mcp__playwright__browser_snapshot()

     // 5. Evaluate JavaScript state
     mcp__playwright__browser_evaluate({
       function: "() => JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('progress') || '{}')"
     })

     // 6. Check console for errors
     mcp__playwright__browser_console_messages()

     // 7. Close browser when done
     mcp__playwright__browser_close()
     ```

     ## Data Schema

     The app expects JSON files with this structure:

     ```typescript
     interface Flashcard {
       word: string;              // Chinese character (e.g., "你")
       word_hanyupinyin: string;  // Pinyin with tone marks (e.g., "nǐ")
       word_english: string;      // English translation (e.g., "you")
       sentence: string;          // Chinese sentence (e.g., "你好！")
       sentence_hanyupinyin: string; // Sentence pinyin (e.g., "Nǐ hǎo!")
       sentence_english: string;  // English translation (e.g., "Hello!")
     }

     type FlashcardList = Flashcard[];
     ```

     ## localStorage Keys

     - `flashcards` - Array of flashcard objects
     - `progress` - Object mapping word to progress data:
       ```javascript
       {
         "你": {
           "read": {
             "intervalIndex": 2,
             "nextReview": 1699999999999,
             "successCount": 3,
             "failCount": 1
           },
           "write": {
             "intervalIndex": 1,
             "nextReview": 1699999999999,
             "successCount": 1,
             "failCount": 0
           }
         }
       }
       ```

     ## Key Features

     ### Two Test Modes

     1. **READ Mode** (blue)
        - Shows: Chinese word + sentence
        - User: Pronounces sentence using speech recognition
        - On success: Confetti + reveal pinyin/English
        - On "I don't know": Text-to-speech reads sentence (0.3x then 0.6x speed)

     2. **WRITE Mode** (purple)
        - Shows: English + pinyin upfront
        - User: Recalls Chinese characters
        - "Show answer": Reveals Chinese characters
        - Self-grade: Correct/Wrong buttons

     ### Session Stats
     - Tracks correct/wrong counts per session (resets on page reload)
     - Displayed on homepage

     ### Import Behavior
     - New cards are shuffled (Fisher-Yates algorithm)
     - Duplicate words (by `word` field) are skipped
     - Data persists in localStorage

     ## Browser APIs Used

     - **Web Speech API** - `SpeechRecognition` for speech-to-text (Chinese)
     - **Web Speech API** - `SpeechSynthesis` for text-to-speech
     - **localStorage** - Data persistence
     - **Canvas API** - Confetti animation

     ## Browser Compatibility

     - Best in Chrome (full speech recognition support)
     - Speech recognition may not work in Safari/Firefox

     # Spaced Repetition Algorithm

     ## Overview

     This app uses a simplified spaced repetition system inspired by SM-2. Cards are scheduled for review at increasing intervals based on successful recall.

     ## Interval Levels

     There are 8 interval levels (0-7):

     | Level | Interval | Description |
     |-------|----------|-------------|
     | 0 | 1 minute | Initial/Reset |
     | 1 | 10 minutes | First success |
     | 2 | 1 hour | Second success |
     | 3 | 1 day | Third success |
     | 4 | 3 days | Fourth success (considered "mastered") |
     | 5 | 7 days | Fifth success |
     | 6 | 14 days | Sixth success |
     | 7 | 30 days | Maximum interval |

     ```javascript
     const INTERVALS = [
       1 * 60 * 1000,           // 1 minute
       10 * 60 * 1000,          // 10 minutes
       60 * 60 * 1000,          // 1 hour
       24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,     // 1 day
       3 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000, // 3 days
       7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000, // 7 days
       14 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,// 14 days
       30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000 // 30 days
     ];
     ```

     ## Scheduling Logic

     ### On Correct Answer
     1. Increment `successCount`
     2. Advance `intervalIndex` by 1 (max 7)
     3. Set `nextReview` = now + INTERVALS[intervalIndex]

     ### On Wrong Answer
     1. Increment `failCount`
     2. Reset `intervalIndex` to 0
     3. Set `nextReview` = now + 1 minute

     ## Card Selection

     Cards are selected for review using this priority:

     1. **Due cards first**: Cards where `nextReview <= now`
     2. **New cards**: Cards with no progress data (treated as due)
     3. **Oldest first**: Among due cards, prioritize by `nextReview` timestamp
     4. **Random tiebreaker**: If multiple cards have same priority, pick randomly

     ```javascript
     function getNextScheduledCard() {
       const now = Date.now();
       let candidates = [];

       flashcards.forEach(card => {
         const p = progress[card.word] || { read: null, write: null };

         // Check read mode
         const readNext = p.read?.nextReview || 0;
         if (readNext <= now) {
           candidates.push({ card, mode: 'read', priority: readNext });
         }

         // Check write mode
         const writeNext = p.write?.nextReview || 0;
         if (writeNext <= now) {
           candidates.push({ card, mode: 'write', priority: writeNext });
         }
       });

       // Sort by priority (oldest first)
       candidates.sort((a, b) => a.priority - b.priority);

       // Random selection among top priority cards
       const topCandidates = candidates.filter(c => c.priority === candidates[0].priority);
       return topCandidates[Math.floor(Math.random() * topCandidates.length)];
     }
     ```

     ## Separate Tracking for Read/Write

     Each card has independent progress for READ and WRITE modes:

     ```javascript
     progress[word] = {
       read: {
         intervalIndex: 0,    // Current level (0-7)
         nextReview: timestamp, // When card is due
         successCount: 0,     // Total correct answers
         failCount: 0         // Total wrong answers
       },
       write: {
         intervalIndex: 0,
         nextReview: timestamp,
         successCount: 0,
         failCount: 0
       }
     }
     ```

     This means:
     - You might master reading a character but still be learning to write it
     - Each mode progresses independently through the interval levels
     - Stats (due/practiced/mastered) are calculated separately per mode

     ## "Mastered" Definition

     A card is considered "mastered" when `intervalIndex >= 4` (3+ days interval). This is used for the homepage stats display.

     ## Initial State

     New cards (no progress data):
     - `nextReview` defaults to 0, meaning immediately due
     - Both READ and WRITE modes start as due
     - Cards are shuffled on import for variety

       <table>

       <div class="highlight">

         <ol>
           <li>Increment <code>successCount</code></li>
           <li>Advance <code>intervalIndex</code> by 1 (max 7)</li>
           <li>Set <code>nextReview</code> = now + interval for new level</li>
         </ol>
       </div>

       <div class="highlight" style="background:#ffebee;">
         <h3 style="color:#f44336; margin-top:0;">On Wrong Answer</h3>
         <ol>
           <li>Increment <code>failCount</code></li>
           <li>Reset <code>intervalIndex</code> to 0</li>
           <li>Set <code>nextReview</code> = now + 1 minute</li>
         </ol>
       </div>

       <h2>Card Selection Priority</h2>
       <p>Cards are selected for review using this priority:</p>
       <ol>
         <li><strong>Due cards first</strong>: Cards where <code>nextReview &lt;= now</code></li>
         <li><strong>New cards</strong>: Cards with no progress data (treated as due)</li>
         <li><strong>Oldest first</strong>: Among due cards, prioritize by <code>nextReview</code> timestamp</li>
         <li><strong>Random tiebreaker</strong>: If multiple cards have same priority, pick randomly</li>
       </ol>

       <h2>Separate Tracking for Read/Write</h2>
       <p>Each card has independent progress for READ and WRITE modes:</p>
       <pre><code>progress[word] = {
       read: {
         intervalIndex: 0,      // Current level (0-7)
         nextReview: timestamp, // When card is due
         successCount: 0,       // Total correct answers
         failCount: 0           // Total wrong answers
       },
       write: {
         intervalIndex: 0,
         nextReview: timestamp,
         successCount: 0,
         failCount: 0
       }
     }</code></pre>

       <p>This means:</p>
       <ul>
         <li>You might master reading a character but still be learning to write it</li>
         <li>Each mode progresses independently through the interval levels</li>
         <li>Stats (due/practiced/mastered) are calculated separately per mode</li>
       </ul>

       <h2>"Mastered" Definition</h2>
       <p>A card is considered <strong>"mastered"</strong> when <code>intervalIndex &gt;= 4</code> (3+ days interval). This is displayed in the homepage stats.</p>

       <h2>Initial State</h2>
       <p>For new cards (no progress data):</p>
       <ul>
         <li><code>nextReview</code> defaults to 0, meaning immediately due</li>
         <li>Both READ and WRITE modes start as due</li>
         <li>Cards are shuffled on import for variety</li>
       </ul>

     </body>
     </html>

  Game screen footer (lines 819-829):
  function updateGameStats() {
    const file = getCurrentFile();
    if (!file) return;
    const p = file.progress[currentCard.word] || { read: {}, write: {} };
    const modeProgress = p[currentMode] || {};
    document.getElementById('gameStats').innerHTML = `
      <span>Success: ${modeProgress.successCount || 0}</span>
      <span>Fail: ${modeProgress.failCount || 0}</span>
      <span>Level: ${(modeProgress.intervalIndex || 0) + 1}/8</span>
    `;
  }

  This shows card-specific data (successCount, failCount, intervalIndex for the current card's current mode).

∴ Thinking…

  The user is asking about the matching logic. Let me look at the checkSpeechMatch function to see how it currently works.

  From what I read earlier:
  function checkSpeechMatch(transcript) {
    const expected = normalizeText(currentCard.sentence);
    const actual = normalizeText(transcript);
    if (actual.includes(expected) || (expected.includes(actual) && actual.length >= expected.length * 0.8)) {
      stopSpeechRecognition();
      handleReadSuccess();
    }
  }

  So currently:
  - actual.includes(expected) - checks if the spoken text contains the expected sentence anywhere
  - expected.includes(actual) && actual.length >= 0.8 * expected.length - checks if the expected contains the actual and actual is at least 80% of expected length

  Given the scenario "ABF.. ABCD" where expected is "ABCD":
  - After normalization (removes punctuation/spaces), actual might be "ABFABCD"
  - actual.includes(expected) → "ABFABCD".includes("ABCD") → true!

  So it would actually match because the actual contains the expected as a substring. This works for the user's scenario.

  But the user is asking about suffix comparison specifically. The current logic does work for "ABF.. ABCD" because "ABFABCD" includes "ABCD". 

  However, the user might be thinking of cases where:
  - Expected: "ABCD"
  - Spoken: "XYZABCD" (contains ABCD, so it matches - this is fine)
  - Spoken: "ABCXYZ" (doesn't contain ABCD, so it won't match - but user finished with wrong ending)

  Actually wait, let me re-read. The user says "ABF.. ABCD" meaning they started wrong, then corrected and said the full sentence properly. After normalization, the transcript 
  would be something like "ABFABCD" (the accumulation of both attempts).

  I see two potential matching strategies with different implications. The current method allows flexibility by checking if the expected text is contained anywhere in the 
  transcript. This approach accommodates mid-sentence corrections and partial attempts. 

  A suffix-based approach would be more restrictive, ensuring the transcript definitively ends with the expected text. This could provide stricter validation but might be less 
  forgiving of minor speech variations or correction attempts.

  The key consideration is balancing accuracy with user-friendliness in speech recognition matching. I'll carefully check the transcript's ending to verify the user spoke the 
  correct sentence. The suffix comparison ensures they finished with the right content, allowing for corrections or false starts. By using endsWith(), I can validate that the 
  critical part of the sentence is spoken correctly, even if additional words precede it. 

  The strategy involves two key checks: first confirming the transcript ends with the expected text, and then explicitly checking for an exact match. This approach provides 
  flexibility while maintaining precision in speech recognition verification.

  The implementation focuses on normalizing both the expected and actual text before comparing, which helps handle variations in capitalization or minor differences. The goal is 
  to create a robust matching mechanism that supports natural speech patterns.

  By using endsWith, the function allows flexibility in how the user completes the sentence, ensuring they can correct or modify their speech while still matching the core 
  expected text.

  Done. Now the matching uses endsWith() which means:
  - "ABFABCD" ends with "ABCD" → ✓ success
  - "ABCD" ends with "ABCD" → ✓ success

⏺ Done. Now using endsWith() for suffix matching:                                                                                      01:35 PM  claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929

  - "ABCD".endsWith("ABCD") → ✓ success (exact match)
  - "ABCDXYZ".endsWith("ABCD") → ✗ no match (extra words at end)

  This is cleaner - the user must finish with the correct sentence, allowing any false starts or corrections before it.

> regarding "Speech now accumulates properly", given "ABCD", if the User corrects themselves midway and repeat the first few words again and finishes the sentence properly 
this time, e.g. "ABF.. ABCD", how does the comparison consider it successful? i.e. does it compare by suffix? or would it be considered a fail? i think suffix comparison is 
best? 

⏺ Good question. Let me check the current matching logic:

⏺ Read(claude/index.html)
  ⎿  Read 15 lines

⏺ Currently it uses actual.includes(expected) which would work for "ABFABCD" containing "ABCD", but you're right - suffix matching is cleaner and more semantically correct
  (they finished with the right sentence).

⏺ Update(claude/index.html)
  ⎿  Updated claude/index.html with 2 additions and 1 removal                                                                
       912        function checkSpeechMatch(transcript) {
       913          const expected = normalizeText(currentCard.sentence);
       914          const actual = normalizeText(transcript);
       915 -        if (actual.includes(expected) || (expected.includes(actual) && actual.length >= expected.length * 0.8)) {
       915 +        // Suffix match: allows corrections like "ABF.. ABCD" to match "ABCD"
       916 +        if (actual.endsWith(expected)) {
       917            stopSpeechRecognition();
       918            handleReadSuccess();
       919          }

⏺ Done. Now using endsWith() for suffix matching:

  - "ABFABCD".endsWith("ABCD") → ✓ success (correction worked)
  - "ABCD".endsWith("ABCD") → ✓ success (exact match)
  - "ABCDXYZ".endsWith("ABCD") → ✗ no match (extra words at end)

  This is cleaner - the user must finish with the correct sentence, allowing any false starts or corrections before it.

  ⎿  Wrote 44 lines to claude/README.md                                                 
     # Chinese Flashcards
     A static web app for learning Chinese characters using spaced repetition.
     **Live Demo:** https://chinese-flashcards.netlify.app
     ## Features
     - **Two test modes**: READ (speech recognition) and WRITE (self-grading)
     - **Spaced repetition**: Cards scheduled at increasing intervals (1 min to 30 days)
     … +34 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Done! Created README.md with:
  - Live demo link
  - Key features summary
  - Quick usage guide
  - JSON format example
  - Links to documentation
  - Browser support note

> mention the os & browsers best supported due to what reasons 

⏺ Update(claude/README.md)
  ⎿  Updated claude/README.md with 12 additions and 2 removals                                                                                                             
       38    - [How it works](SPACED_REPETITION.html) - Spaced repetition algorithm
       39    - [Developer Guide](DEVELOPER.html) - Technical details
       40    
       41 -  ## Browser Support
       41 +  ## Browser & OS Support
       42    
       43 -  Best in Chrome (full speech recognition support). Speech recognition may not work in Safari/Firefox.
       43 +  **Recommended:** Chrome on macOS, Windows, or Android
       44 +  
       45 +  | Feature | Chrome | Safari | Firefox |
       46 +  |---------|--------|--------|---------|
       47 +  | Speech Recognition (READ mode) | Full | Limited | No |
       48 +  | Text-to-Speech (Chinese) | Full | Full | Full |
       49 +  | localStorage | Full | Full | Full |
       50 +  
       51 +  **Why Chrome?** The Web Speech API (`SpeechRecognition`) for Chinese speech-to-text is fully supported only in Chrome. Safari has limited support, and Firefox
          +  does not support it at all.
       52 +  
       53 +  **Mobile:** Works on Android Chrome. iOS Safari has limited speech recognition support.

⏺ Done! Updated the Browser & OS Support section with:
  - Recommended setup (Chrome on macOS/Windows/Android)
  - Feature compatibility table
  - Explanation of Web Speech API limitations
  - Mobile notes (Android Chrome vs iOS Safari)

       51    **Why Chrome?** The Web Speech API (`SpeechRecognition`) for Chinese speech-to-text is fully supported only in Chrome. Safari has limited support, and Firefox
